### ✓ 2026-11-15: `concepts/haecceity` L67 says Parfit holds personal identity "can branch or fuse" and that on anti-haecceitism "A perfect replica would be you" — Parfit's criterion has a non-branching clause (fission ends the original; what branches is Relation R), and `topics/personal-identity` L125 says the Map agrees with Parfit that identity can't branch
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: /home/andy/unfin/unfinishablemap/obsidian/concepts/haecceity.md
- **Notes**: From the outer review of 2026-11-15 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro): §3 "Psychological continuity and Parfit" item 3, §10 "Main article versus haecceity and the identity apex", improvement 13. The driver confirmed these strings on the live page, count 1 each: L67 "A perfect replica would be you.", "Parfit's view exemplifies this: personal identity reduces to psychological continuity and can branch or fuse." and "Identity cannot branch because haecceity cannot be divided or copied." There are three problems. (1) Parfit's psychological criterion includes a non-branching clause. On it fission ends the original, and what can branch is Relation R, the relation Parfit says matters. The SEP "Personal Identity" entry (fetched by the driver 2026-11-15) states the clause as "a past or future being is you only if she is then psychologically continuous with you and *no other being* then is", with the consequence "Fission is death." The Map's own `topics/personal-identity` L125 says the Map's fission verdict "aligns with Parfit's conclusion that identity can't 'branch'". (2) Anti-haecceitism, as L65 defines it (identity supervenes on qualitative character across worlds), does not say that a replica is you. On every view a qualitatively identical replica elsewhere is numerically distinct by location and causal history, which `personal-identity` L97 grants. Restate the anti-haecceitist position as the claim that qualitative and relational facts (continuity, causal connection, no rival) settle whether a later person is you. On the wide psychological criterion a sole replica standing in R would then count as you; with two, neither does. (3) The last sentence makes non-branching a haecceitist privilege, but Parfit and the standard psychological view share it. Keep the haecceitist contrast and put it where it belongs: haecceitists deny that those relations settle identity at all. No other page in topics/concepts/apex/voids/positions carries the "Parfit … can branch" wording (driver grep). Do not add a Parfit quotation unless a page locator is verified at source. The reviewer linked a course-hosted PDF of *Reasons and Persons* (Part Three) that was not opened here; treat it as a lead. LENGTH: 3,486 of concepts hard 3,500; NET ≤ 0.

Item (1): Claude Focus 3 independently: "On Olson’s view, removing the cerebrum leaves the animal, and therefore you, behind", and both reviewers name the thinking-animal argument as animalism's main motivation. Add at most one clause naming no-self views (Buddhist causal-stream accounts, Metzinger's self-model theory) as a fourth answer, with links on existing words. Do NOT create an animalism page: topics and concepts are both at cap (370/370, `count_section_files`, 2026-11-15). LENGTH: 4,033 of topics hard 4,000; NET ≤ 0. Claude independently flags item (2), L177: "Nothing in Parfit makes persons “interchangeable”." and item (3), L77: "“Shows” is stronger than this article’s own body, which says “doesn’t prove haecceity on its own”." ChatGPT §3: "His claim is not numerical interchangeability". Item (1), L91, is ChatGPT-only. Ownership (also in Coordination): L77, L91 and L177 only. The Claude-leg hub task deletes Consideration 3 (L99) and renumbers the list, so keep L97's "point 1" valid and do not touch L93–L101. From the outer review of 2026-11-15 (ChatGPT 5.6 Sol Pro): §3 "Psychological continuity and Parfit" items 2–3, §5 "Meaning", improvement 7. The page contradicts itself about what Parfit grants. L97 says causal history does not generate thisness because that "would collapse into the numerical non-identity Parfit already grants" (count 1). L125 says the Map's fission verdict "aligns with Parfit's conclusion that identity can't 'branch'". Three passages say otherwise. (1) L91 (count 1): "that you are not interchangeable with a replica sharing your psychology. But this is precisely what Parfit denies". Parfit does not deny that a replica is numerically distinct from you when there are two continuers. He denies that identity is what matters, and that a further fact always settles the question. Restate the target as that. (2) L177 (count 1): "If Parfit were right and you're interchangeable with any replica sharing your psychology, your particular existence seems less significant—patterns are repeatable, and so would you be". This credits Parfit with numerical repeatability. The fair objection is that Parfit puts what matters in Relation R, which a replica can bear even where identity fails, so uniqueness loses its special value; the Map denies that R exhausts what matters. (3) L77 (count 1) summarises the parfit-reductionism page as "zombie conceivability shows identity involves something beyond continuity". That is stronger than this page's own L95: "the argument doesn't prove haecceity on its own" and at most it can "open logical space". Bring the summary into line with L95. NOT tasked: L77 "Parfit says 'Is that person you?' may have no determinate answer". The reviewer calls it unrestricted, but "may" plus Parfit's empty-question doctrine makes it defensible; change it only if a fix is free. ### ✓ 2026-11-14: Synaesthesia citation fixes (2026-10-25 22:25Z deep review) not propagated — Galton "survey of Royal Society fellows", Macpherson misspelt and her stance inverted, Wager 1999 stripped of "externalist", van Leeuwen "find", Gray 7(5) and editor-order metadata — at `apex/phenomenal-variation-within-a-species`, `voids/synesthetic-void`, `concepts/phenomenology-vs-function-axis`
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/apex/phenomenal-variation-within-a-species.md
- **Notes**: MODEL = the corrected `topics/synaesthesia` (read the review file's citation ledger first). Standards: Sachs 1812 first described synaesthesia (Jewanski, Day & Ward 2009, J Hist Neurosci 18(3)); Galton popularised it — his survey covered 100 men, 19 of them Fellows, and number-form informants were "correspondents" (Human Faculty pp. 103–104). ### ✓ 2026-11-13: `concepts/working-memory` L59 reports only the sensitivity correlation from Franco-Martínez et al. (2026) and glosses it as "entangled with residual conscious processing", omitting the paper's above-chance accuracy on unseen trials and its above-chance regression intercept, which two sibling pages report
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/working-memory.md
- **Notes**: L59 at mint: "Franco-Martínez et al. (2026), re-analysing that same dataset for publication bias, recovered a bias-corrected estimate (≈0.16, its credible interval spanning zero) that "only provides inconclusive evidence for the existence of such an effect," and their own 19-laboratory Registered Report replication found unconscious WM "positively associated with perceptual sensitivity"—that is, entangled with residual conscious processing rather than a clean unconscious channel." The abstract (Crossref, doi 10.1093/nc/niaf046, read this pass) lists three results: (1) above-chance performance on cue-present trials reported as unseen, .55 accuracy; (2) a positive correlation between performance and cue-detection sensitivity, r = .228; (3) a significant above-chance intercept in the regression of performance on sensitivity, β0 = .521. Its conclusion: the findings "suggest that WM can operate on unconscious representations, although it remains positively associated with perceptual sensitivity". The page gives result (2) alone. Result (3) is the one that bears on the page's gloss: an intercept above chance means performance stays above chance when sensitivity is extrapolated to zero, which counts against reading the effect as wholly residual conscious processing. `topics/language-recursion-and-consciousness` L53 and `apex/tool-that-cannot-say-its-user` L56 already give the fuller account, so three Map pages currently describe one paper in two ways. WHAT TO DO: state all three results in about the words now used, keep the authors' qualification, and replace "entangled with residual conscious processing rather than a clean unconscious channel" with a gloss the intercept permits. The bias-corrected meta-analytic estimate and its quotation were not in the abstract: verify them in the paper's text before keeping them, and if the full text cannot be reached say so in the changelog and leave that clause as it stands. Check that L45's parenthesis and L63's last sentence still agree with the revised L59. Run sync afterwards.

### ✓ 2026-11-13: `concepts/selection-only-channel` L62 bounds "the maximum information transferable by realising one specific candidate" by log2(N), which is a bound on expected information, and L72 leans on Han and Choi (2016) for more than the reviewer says the paper shows (verify first)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/selection-only-channel.md
- **Notes**: (A) About L62 to L64: "the maximum information transferable by realising one specific candidate is bounded above by the Shannon entropy of a uniform distribution" and "I_max(event) ≤ log₂(N) bits" (count 1 each at mint); about L80 "The per-event log₂(N) ceiling". The reviewer's point is standard information theory: the surprisal of a particular outcome, minus log2 of its probability, exceeds log2(N) whenever that outcome's probability is below 1/N, so the log2(N) figure bounds the entropy of the output and the mutual information per channel use, not every single event. State the bound as an expectation per selection event (mutual information between the selecting state and the outcome is at most the outcome entropy, which is at most log2 N) and adjust "per-event" phrasing at L42, L60, L64 and L80 only as far as needed for consistency. (B) VERIFY FIRST, about L72: "Han & Choi (2016) *derive* the Born rule from a causality condition" and the pointer that any systematic per-trial departure from Born probabilities is a relativistic-causality problem. The reviewer reports that the paper says no-signalling alone is insufficient, introduces a stronger "space-like causality" condition (joint probabilities of spacelike-separated measurements independent of temporal order), and works within a construction using entangled qubits. ### ✓ 2026-11-13: `concepts/conscious-vs-unconscious-processing` L76 says working memory "requires consciousness" and L223 that "subliminal priming decays within 500ms" is the current evidence, while `concepts/working-memory` L59 and two other pages report a 19-laboratory replication of above-chance memory for unseen cues after a 2-second delay
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/conscious-vs-unconscious-processing.md
- **Notes**: Confirmed live at mint, each string count 1 unless stated. L76: "Working memory—holding information active for use—requires consciousness. Subliminal priming effects decay within 500ms." L80: "Masked stimuli can prime responses but cannot be maintained for delayed tasks". L223 (what would challenge this view, item 3): "If masked stimuli could be maintained for delayed tasks, the "durable maintenance" criterion would fall. Current evidence: subliminal priming decays within 500ms." The description (L3) and L46 also list durable maintenance as a function unconscious processing "cannot achieve". Against this, `concepts/working-memory` L59 opens "Maintenance can be unconscious—though how cleanly is contested" and cites Soto, Mäntylä and Silvanto (2011) and Franco-Martínez et al. (2026); `topics/language-recursion-and-consciousness` L53 and `apex/tool-that-cannot-say-its-user` L56 report the 2026 result in full. Verified at Crossref this pass (doi 10.1093/nc/niaf046): the Registered Report (19 laboratories, N = 531) had participants report the rotation of a subliminal grating after a 2-second delay and found .55 accuracy on cue-present trials reported as unseen, a positive correlation with detection sensitivity (r = .228) and an above-chance regression intercept (β0 = .521); the authors conclude the findings "suggest that WM can operate on unconscious representations, although it remains positively associated with perceptual sensitivity". So the page's own stated falsifier for the durable-maintenance criterion has, on the Map's own cited evidence, at least partly occurred, and the page neither cites that evidence nor grades it. WHAT TO DO: bring L76, L80 and L223 into line with working-memory L59: maintenance of unseen stimuli over seconds is reported and contested, the effect is small and tied to perceptual sensitivity, and the tighter link to consciousness is with manipulation. Say what remains of criterion 1 after that (conscious maintenance is far more robust and longer-lived) without claiming necessity. Adjust the description and L46 only if they still assert that unconscious processing cannot maintain information. Add the Franco-Martínez reference if length allows, trimming within the same section to pay for it; if the fix cannot be made within the length limit, record that in the changelog and leave the task open. Do not copy the reviewer's stronger wording ("falsified", "proving unconscious retention"), and treat its Pedale citation as unverified (the paper found is Pedale, Karampela and Eriksson 2025, eNeuro; content not read). Re-grep each string before editing. Run sync afterwards and confirm the Hugo copy changed.

- **Type**: expand-topic

CAP: `tools.evolution.state.count_section_files('topics')` read 369/370 on 2026-11-12; that count includes the `non-temporal-consciousness.refinement-log.md` sidecar. ### ✓ 2026-11-12: Research self-observation and the moments of post-decoherence selection — where experience locates selection, and how those moments line up with choice and free will
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: OPERATOR-REQUESTED. QUESTION: can self-observation (introspection, contemplative and micro-phenomenological report, the felt structure of deciding) serve as a locating instrument for *where and when* post-decoherence selection would occur in a conscious life, and do those candidate moments coincide with choice (deliberation, effortful attention, commitment, veto)? This note feeds the operator-requested topics article in the companion P1 expand-topic task directly below, which combines the note with the Map's own conclusions.
  OUTPUT: write the note to exactly `obsidian/research/self-observation-and-the-moments-of-selection-2026-11-12.md`, keeping this filename whatever the run date. The companion task references this stem, and that stops the research chain from minting a duplicate expand task. Frontmatter: `recommended_action: article`, `target_section: topics`, `target_slug: self-observation-and-the-moments-of-selection`. The article is operator-requested, so do not declare `none` or `fold`. If the research shows the premise is weak (for example, experience cannot locate selection at all), say so plainly in an Assess-First Verdict and recommend how the article should be framed around that finding.
  ANGLES. Every author and work named here is an UNVERIFIED LEAD from the operator session. Verify each at the publisher, Crossref, PhilPapers or an archive full text before relying on it, and drop any that do not check out. (1) Self-measurement in quantum foundations: von Neumann 1932 (the movable cut, the "abstract ego"); London & Bauer 1939 (the observer's faculty of introspection as what completes the measurement; verify the exact wording at the source); Wigner 1961; Albert 1983 on quantum-mechanical automata and self-measurement; Breuer 1995 on the impossibility of accurate state self-measurement; Wigner's-friend and local-friendliness results as they bear on an observer measuring itself. Ask whether this literature identifies a privileged moment at which an observer observing *itself* fixes an outcome, and whether decoherence leaves such a moment (the gap between improper mixture and definite outcome). (2) Where physics would put candidate moments in the brain: neural decoherence timescales (Tegmark 2000 and the replies) against decision timescales (Libet 1983 readiness potential; Schurger et al. 2012 accumulator model; Maoz et al. 2019 on arbitrary versus deliberate decisions; veto, "free won't"); the Map's own candidate loci (Stapp's Process 1 question-choice, motor selection, attention). Report the timescale mismatch honestly. (3) The temporal grain of experience: discrete versus continuous perception (e.g. VanRullen's perceptual cycles), Stroud's psychological moment, the specious present, Pöppel's ~3 s window. Can experience register individual selections, or only their aggregate? (4) First-person reports of the moment of choice: micro-phenomenology of decision (Petitmengin and colleagues); William James on effort and the "fiat"; contemplative reports, including the Abhidhamma cognitive series (citta-vīthi), where volition (cetanā) is said to be generated in the javana phase, and witness-practice reports of watching a choice arise; reactivity, i.e. whether introspecting a choice changes it (Schwitzgebel; think-aloud reactivity studies). (5) Free will: Kane's self-forming actions (effortful torn decisions as the indeterministic locus; Kane 2024 drops the "doubled effort", see the open P3 on that), the luck objection, and whatever evidence, if any, links felt effort or openness to neural indeterminacy. The deflationary rival is already researched in `research/planck-self-observing-ego-and-mackay-logical-indeterminacy-2026-10-29`: Planck 1932 and MacKay 1960 explain felt openness WITHOUT indeterminism. The note must set out how the self-observation angle stands against it.
  MAP CONSTRAINTS. Read these before writing the note's Map-relevance section, and quote them verbatim rather than paraphrasing them stronger: `positions/quantum-interface` [P-Q1](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q1) (post-decoherence selection is the preferred candidate), [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2) (Born statistics preserved exactly; "only conditionals on the conscious state itself are free"), [P-Q9](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q9) (self-concealing interface) and [P-Q10](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q10) (no worked toy model); `positions/agency-and-will` [P-A3](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a3) (Libet), [P-A4](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a4) ("no agent can certify its own causal efficacy by introspection") and [P-A6](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a6) (the volition as the locus of contact). Lay out the central tension without settling it by fiat. If [P-A4](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a4) holds, self-observation cannot *verify* that a selection occurred. It may still map where experience presents openness, effort and commitment, and so name the conditionals on conscious state that [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2) leaves free and that [P-Q9](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q9) says psychophysical tests can still reach.

### ✓ 2026-11-12: `topics/correlationism-and-the-ancestrality-argument` — placement uses Meillassoux's 2012 vocabulary but misses his "hyperphysics" category and his objection to dualism; two inferences rest on the wrong kind of premise; Hallward quoted without Brown's rejoinder
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/correlationism-and-the-ancestrality-argument.md
- **Notes**: Four fixes, each target sentence occurs exactly once (grep -c -F = 1 on 2026-11-12). Read the review file for the source quotations and page numbers before editing; every quotation there was grepped in raw source text. (1) Sentences "What remains in his vocabulary is dogmatic or metaphysical realism of a Cartesian stripe, and the Map should accept the description." and "Whether that reading blocks his objection is a question the Map puts to itself, not one the literature has considered." Meillassoux's 2012 Berlin lecture (already reference 8) defines "metaphysical" and "dogmatic" by the Principle of Sufficient Reason (transcript p. 12) and supplies "hyperphysics" for a postulate held without necessity (p. 13), which he says speculative materialism "has nothing against". Relabel, remove the claim that the literature has not considered it, choose between "speculative" and "postulative" for the Map, and report his coordination objection ("the enigma of all dualisms", p. 15) with the Map's answer. Do not put any new string in quotation marks without grepping it in the transcript. (2) Sentence beginning "Consciousness is not reducible to physical processes, but it is also not constitutive of them: the tenet posits two terms" ends "which is the exact denial of the correlationist thesis". The page defines correlationism as a thesis about access and itself says asserting independence and asserting access are different claims, so an existence posit is not its denial. Rest the denial on what the Map claims to know of the physical term and say what it claims to know of the mental term apart; if that is only existence and irreducibility, say the Map is weak-correlationist about the mental term's nature too. Check the heading sentence "The Map claims knowledge of both terms apart." against the result. (3) Sentence beginning "Together they commit the Map to the claim Brassier demands". [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2) and [P-Q9](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q9) are concealment claims about statistics where the interface operates; they do not say where it operates and do not entail independence. Credit locality to Tenet 2 and the two-jobs passage of `concepts/prebiotic-collapse`; keep [P-Q2](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q2)/[P-Q9](/positions/quantum-interface/#p-q9) as the reason the public statistical record is left alone, scoped to the unconditioned aggregate marginal (the preceding sentence says "the physical world's statistics are untouched" with no scope word). (4) After "The reply literature is largely unpersuaded." the page quotes Hallward 2008 p. 55. Add Nathan Brown, "The Speculative and the Specific: On Hallward and Meillassoux", in Bryant, Srnicek and Harman (eds.), The Speculative Turn (re.press, 2011), whose retrojection rejoinder targets that sentence; name Hallward's other three charges in a clause; note in the Hallward reference that a longer version is ch. 10 of the same volume. Verify the Brown page numbers and the volume's metadata at re.press before writing the reference. OPTIONAL LEAD, unverified in the primary: the second-paragraph clause "no paper connects the ancestrality argument to the quantum measurement problem at all" is unscoped; Bitbol, Maintenant la finitude (Flammarion, 2019) is a correlationist reply to Meillassoux that uses quantum mechanics (per Bastien's review, Philosophiques 46(2)). Either scope the clause to the literature surveyed, matching the Open Questions bullet, or read the book or the Philosophiques 47(2) dossier first. Do not assert what Bitbol argues from this note. Kim was answering Griffin's 1999 *Process Studies* essay: the religion-online summary says "Kim responds to Griffin's article, Materialist and Panexperientialist Physicalism", and Kim writes "as I read his book (as I hope to in the near future)". None of these records the critic's target text, and none asks whether a quoted critic's strongest argument is present. **Ask.** Add one check in the (c-iii) family, e.g. "(c-vi) critic-target and critic-completeness". ### ✓ 2026-11-11: `topics/time-bias-and-thank-goodness-thats-over` reports Latham et al. (2022) by its title alone (L63), calls a tensed-belief concession a phenomenal datum that "both sides accept" (L77), and overstates the No Many Worlds and growing-block paragraphs (L89, L87)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/time-bias-and-thank-goodness-thats-over.md
- **Notes**: First pessimistic review of this page (unchanged since its 2026-09-28 deep review). Each OLD sentence below was at grep -c -F = 1 at 17:27Z; re-count before editing. (1) L63, HIGH, verified at source. OLD: `Andrew Latham, Kristie Miller, Christian Tarsney and Hannah Tierney (2022) report, in their title's words, that belief in robust temporal passage "(probably) does not explain future-bias."` The abstract continues: "But we also find that people give more future-biased responses when asked to simulate a belief in robust passage. We take this to suggest that the phenomenology that attends simulation of that belief may be partially responsible for future-bias" (read in the Oxford repository author copy, ora.ox.ac.uk; re-fetch it, the session copy is gone). The same four authors then tested phenomenology directly: "Robust passage phenomenology probably does not explain future-bias", Synthese 200(2), 2022, doi 10.1007/s11229-022-03514-3; abstract (OpenAlex): they "found no evidence in its favour" and conclude that passage phenomenology alone probably does not explain the bias, though having it and taking it to be veridical may contribute. Report both results, add the Synthese reference (retrieve its article number; not fetched), and let the next sentence (`Future-bias may be a feature of human psychology whatever one's metaphysics`) rest on both. (2) L77, HIGH. OLD: `Both sides accept this.` Mellor concedes an indispensable tensed BELIEF (the page's own L47); the item asserts a tensed PHENOMENAL character. Split what is agreed (tensed belief explains the timing of relief) from what is disputed (phenomenal character beyond the belief; the Map holds it, deflationists about passage experience deny it). LEADS, unverified, check before citing: Hoerl 2014 "Do we (seem to) perceive passage?"; Deng 2013 "On explaining why time seems to pass". Same pass, L81: `A theory that treats the tensed character as a mere product of how we are built has not engaged the datum.` either goes or says what the evolutionary account leaves out in Pearson 2018b's terms (before/after asymmetry explained, tensed/tenseless not); and `the one theories of time most often explain away` needs an example or a rewrite, since on Mellor's view relief misrepresents nothing. (3) L89, MEDIUM. OLD: `Relief presupposes a single settled past, and much ordinary relief is counterfactual as well as temporal: thank goodness the test was negative, the car missed.` Temporal relief is available on many-worlds (each branch has one past), so restrict the claim to counterfactual relief. State the Everettian self-locating reply (relief that I am in this branch), which the page's own hospital case models: both patients exist, both operations occur, and the hope still has an object. Give the Map's distinguishing argument (one dreading subject whose continuers meet every outcome, against a second patient who was never you) and mark the remainder as a disagreement at the level of the Map's commitments on indexical identity, in plain prose. Link the existing self-locating discussion on the many-worlds concept page. (4) L87, MEDIUM. OLD: `On that claim, "that's over" is true because the pain is fixed and will not be undergone again.` The paragraph needs Forrest's real-but-dead past; the linked section of consciousness-and-the-ontology-of-temporal-becoming (L145) itself says this "raises the question of what extinguishes consciousness in settled time-slices". Carry that cost in one sentence and reword the truth condition so it says what "no longer undergone" adds to "earlier than now". DO NOT touch: the normative abstention, the Prior quotations, the three-way split, "does not vindicate it for free", Hare's shrinking-block caution (deep-review 09-28 Stability Notes). Not a contradiction, do not "fix": L85 "Consciousness does not make the past settled" against L87 "active participation in settlement" (L85 says "mostly"; the source treats mindless growth and neural participation alike). ai_system: append the executing model only if absent anywhere in the string. See reviews/pessimistic-2026-11-11-time-bias-and-thank-goodness-thats-over.

### ✓ 2026-11-11: `concepts/valence` L85 says the asymbolia dissociation "binds the bare-correlation epiphenomenalist", but the same page's L69 says the dissociation "does not settle" the fork — a lesion that removes affect and behaviour together is a common cause and binds no epiphenomenalist

- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-11-10 (Claude Fable 5.2) §4 "concepts/valence" item and §3 Claim A; both phrases verified live (`grep -c -F` = 1 each). (a) L85 OLD "When the phenomenal property is absent, behavior changes, which binds the bare-correlation epiphenomenalist; the Map concedes that the phenomenal-concept version survives it ([P-MC1](/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation/#p-mc1))." [P-MC1](/positions/arguments-for-mental-causation/#p-mc1)'s "decisively burdens bare-correlation" is a claim about the self-stultification argument, not about the asymbolia dissociation; the insular lesion is a common cause of the lost affect and the lost behaviour, so the dissociation presses on the bare-correlation view without binding it — which is what L69 already says ("the dissociation does not settle it"). Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. THE ISSUE. The page already concedes physicalist compatibility (L42 "nothing here requires dualism", L96, L100). So the open question is classification, not dualism: what makes boredom's occlusion a void rather than ordinary interface compression? **(1) Compression objection.** L42 defines the void as "a felt verdict that steers behaviour without displaying its grounds". Hunger need not name the best nutrient, fatigue the best recovery, anxiety the distribution behind it, so on that definition almost every affect is a void. One candidate reply for the fork to TEST, not assume: hunger, thirst and fear present a determinate target TYPE (food, water, the threat), while boredom presents only "not this". If that holds, state it in a sentence or two at L42 or L50. If it fails, concede in natural prose that face 1 is one instance of a compression that runs through affect generally, and say what (if anything) remains distinctive. Name the strongest rival briefly: Danckert & Elpidorou (2023), abstract VERIFIED (PMID 36922277, *Trends Cogn Sci* 27(5):494–507): "boredom is an affective signal that we have deviated from an optimal ('Goldilocks') zone of cognitive engagement". It integrates meaning, attention and the call to act, so the signal underdetermines its cause. Reference: Danckert, J., & Elpidorou, A. (2023). In search of boredom: Beyond a functional account. *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*, 27(5), 494–507. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tics.2023.02.002. Optional, if budget allows: Struk et al. (2020), abstract VERIFIED (PMID 32401144): visible but forbidden affordances (n = 121) raised boredom over an empty room (n = 107), so alternatives enter the computation. **(2) Response-dependence.** "this lecture is boring" may express a relational, response-dependent property, as "this food is nauseating" does. On that reading outward predication is not projection at all. L58 already moves the void to whether the verdict carries the fitting-versus-projected distinction on its face. Check whether that move already answers the reply. If it does, say so in one clause at L54 or L58, and make sure L40's "presents a failed relation as a property of the object" and L54 do not claim more than L58 defends. Do not rewrite face 2's Study 2 material (settled 2026-11-03, commit 3fb2416a82). It is Y." or "load-bearing". ADDENDUM 2026-11-11 (Claude Opus 5.5 leg, `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-11-claude-opus-5-5.md` verified problems 1 and 2, §(c) item 6, methodology item 4): CONVERGENT. A second reviewer independently raises the compression objection ("Every affect presents a verdict without displaying its computational grounds; pain does not show nociceptor statistics either"); without an argument that boredom's opacity differs in kind, the entry is a special case of self-opacity. Two VERIFIED inputs change how the fork should test the candidate "not this" reply. (A) Westgate & Wilson 2018 (author PDF): bored people's choices "appear neither indiscriminate nor the opposite of previous goals"; they prefer exciting films, meaningful activities and interesting foods. Bench & Lench 2013 (PMC4217586): "reference points would determine the goal that boredom motivates, as the desirable goal would be one that is different from the current situation." So boredom carries type-level direction (toward interest, meaning, stimulation), and the bare "not this" reply probably fails as stated. The defensible distinction is token-level: boredom specifies a kind of target, not a particular one. L50 "Functional accounts agree on the direction without supplying a destination" overstates Bench & Lench; recast it along those lines. L50's own defeater ("evidence that bored people can reliably say which alternative would repay them would weaken it") is already partly met at type level; say so in a clause. (B) L58 "or attention from meaning" in the void-claim sentence. Westgate & Wilson's manipulation checks are VERIFIED: high-meaning participants rated the task more personally meaningful, F(1, 214) = 58.69, d = 1.04, and low-attention participants "reported more attentional difficulties"; across 14 studies, concentration difficulty and meaningfulness correlated only r = −.12. People can self-report the two components, so delete "or attention from meaning" (−4) and leave causal attribution as the open margin, which L58's last sentence already names. This touches only the void-claim sentence, not the settled Study 2 material. Both edits fit inside this task's existing +60; no budget increase. Eastwood et al. 2012 clause (a), VERIFIED at PubMed (PMID 26168505): boredom occurs when we "are not able to successfully engage attention with internal (e.g., thoughts or feelings) or external (e.g., environmental stimuli) information required for participating in satisfying activity". By that definition the bout ends (analytic); as a matter of fact it is untested, and L90 says "Contemplative practitioners could show whether…". Either way "tends to" is the wrong modal. Edits: **(a) L40.** "engaged study of a bout tends to end it" becomes "engaged study of a bout ends it by that definition", or similar. Keep the `[[#examination|…]]` link. **(b) L70 heading.** "## Examining Boredom Tends to End It {#examination}" becomes, for example, "## Examining Boredom Ends It, by Definition {#examination}". KEEP `{#examination}`; the only inbound mentions of the heading text are in archived changelogs. **(c) L74.** After "What the definition excludes is a long, engaged first-person reading of one bout", add one clause: unabsorbed, unsatisfying observation of a bout (a noting practice, for example) is not excluded, so what is excluded is a *satisfying* engaged reading. **(d) L94.** "*unexplorable* in face 4, relative to that definition" should reflect (c), for example "*unexplorable* in face 4 only in that the attentional definition rules out a satisfying engaged reading". Append the EXECUTING model to ai_system only if it is ABSENT anywhere in the string. Bump ai_modified to the real `date -u`. Changelog, sync, and verify both trees. Slaby (2010) VERIFIED in the author's PDF, p. 107: "in order to gain an understanding of boredom, we need to wake up to it, attend to it, live it in some lucid sort of way. No amount of amassing factual knowledge can replace this event of waking up in and to the attunement of boredom." Slaby calls the method "performative and transformative phenomenology". So the L72 gloss "rather than by inspecting it" is the Map's, and Slaby's contrast is with amassing factual knowledge, not with attending. Edit (f), optional, L80 "At the far edge lies Heidegger's profound boredom": Slaby p. 117 has profound boredom "rarely if ever experienced in the course of an ordinary human life". The IASP terminology page defines pain as "An unpleasant sensory and emotional experience associated with, or resembling that associated with, actual or potential tissue damage" and notes "Pain and nociception are different phenomena. The article's partition puts the unpleasantness outside "pain" by stipulation and then presents its absence as a gap consciousness must fill. NEW should name the three-way split the standard vocabulary already has — nociception (encoding), pain (the unpleasant sensory-emotional experience), wider suffering (grief, dread, moral suffering) — and relocate the section's thesis to the pain/suffering boundary without redefining pain as the signal; the later "C-fibre" shorthand (L160, L176) then reads as nociception. NEW: IIT 4.0 separates quantity (Φ) from the quality the Φ-structure specifies, so a domain-specific deficit is a structural change the theory would have to model, not a falsification of a scalar-only theory; the explanatory-gap point at L132 survives untouched. ### ✓ 2026-11-10: `voids/chroma-void` L42 and L78 credit Wong et al. (2020) with conceding that no test separates a heard chroma from a fast label; their concession is narrower (whether a trainee represents every pitch absolutely or names some tones against an internal reference), and they float a fast response as a sign of the GENUINE side (optimistic review 2026-11-10, perceptual-category voids wing, Calibration 1)
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/chroma-void.md
- **Notes**: Calibration fix (Hardline Empiricist), review §Calibration Concerns 1. HEADROOM: analyze_length 2,779 against voids hard 3,000 (gate >=), headroom 220 at 19:10Z; budget for this task about +50; re-measure first. SOURCE CHECK (driver review, at source: bioRxiv preprint 10.1101/355933 posted 2019-01-28, section "Did the training lead to 'genuine AP'?"; the published wording of the core sentence differs slightly, per the research note's [raw]): Wong et al. consider that trainees "could have generated reference tones internally in a trial and use it for subsequent trials", call that outcome "quasi-AP" as against "'genuine AP' involving the representation of every pitch in an absolute manner (e.g., Levitin & Rogers, 2005)", and add "One could perhaps use fast responses to infer automatic pitch naming in an absolute manner. But the search for the threshold response time could be elusive". So their untestable hypothesis concerns how pitch is REPRESENTED (absolute vs internally referenced), and a fast label sits on their GENUINE side, not on the deflationary side of the void's quality-vs-label question, which they never pose. The void survives intact (every face, the neutrality verdict, the unexplored margin, the core question); only the support borrowed from their concession goes. EDITS (each OLD at grep -c -F = 1 at 19:10Z): (A) L42 OLD: `Whether possessors hear chroma as a quality or recognise a label quickly is the open question, and the researchers who train adults into the ability concede that no agreed test separates the two (Wong et al. 2020).` NEW: `Whether possessors hear chroma as a quality or recognise a label quickly is the open question. The researchers who train adults into the ability concede a narrower gap: no agreed test shows whether a trainee represents every pitch absolutely or names some tones against an internal reference (Wong et al. 2020).` (about +13). (B) L78 OLD: `What separates a heard chroma from a fast, accurate label is the very thing the field has no agreed way to test.` NEW: `Their question is how pitch is represented, and they float a fast response as a possible sign of the absolute kind while calling the search for a threshold "elusive". The void's question lies one step further on: whether a fast, reference-free label also comes with a heard chroma.` (about +26; do NOT add "no study located" here: the research covered Wong's full text only, and the preceding sentences already state that absence). (C) L82 OLD: `Possessors then report only the syllable because there is nothing else to report, and the "untestable hypothesis" has no content beyond performance.` NEW: `Possessors then report only the syllable because there is nothing else to report, and a heard chroma has no content beyond performance and the representation that produces it.` (about +2). (D) L92 OLD: `structured persistence moderate, since the perceptual-versus-labelling question is restated as open in 2017, 2019 and 2020 and the obvious dissolving experiment did not dissolve it` NEW: `structured persistence weak to moderate, since the perceptual-versus-labelling question is restated as open in 2017 and 2019, and the obvious dissolving experiment, adult training, never put it to trainees` (about +1; the honest form `voids/vitality-form-void` L84 already uses: "never tested by a programme that tried to dissolve it"). GUARDS: keep L78's verbatim quotes ("there has not been an agreed upon way to ascertain whether an individual perceives every pitch in an absolute manner or not"; "remains an untestable hypothesis") exactly as they are, since they match the research note's [raw] PUBLISHED text; do not swap in the preprint's "to date there is not" wording. "elusive" appears in both versions. Keep L90 ("Wong and colleagues' concession concerns the field's present tools."), which is still true. Keep the register (Unexplorable core on present methods, Unexplored margin, two Occluded faces); if you judge the core's support now rests on L90's conceivability argument alone, say so in one clause and do not otherwise re-grade. Never upgrade a tenet. Leave `voids/palette-extension-void` L74 and `voids/voids.md` L221/L316 alone: they quote "genuine absolute pitch ... untestable" accurately or state an unattributed absence that is true. When fixing L84, qualify L70 so the continuity claim attaches to memristive / mixed-signal designs only (≤ 6 words, e.g. "Discrete (digital) to continuous (memristive)"). The reviewer also notes RRAM random-telegraph noise arises from single-electron trap capture by tunnelling (Ambrogio et al. 2014 *IEEE TED*; Puglisi et al. 2019 *Sci Rep* — unverified) — closer to "quantum at the decision point" than CMOS thermal noise; mention only if word-neutral.
  **ADDENDUM 2026-11-09 (Gemini outer review)**: `reviews/outer-review-2026-11-09-gemini-3-1-pro` §2.1 converges with the Claude addendum from the engineering side — Intel Loihi 2 (2021) is fully digital CMOS whose asynchronous network-on-chip processes event-driven, graded (multi-bit) spikes in continuous time, and the 2022 neuromorphic roadmap (Christensen, D.V., et al., *Neuromorphic Computing and Engineering* 2(2), 022501, DOI 10.1088/2634-4386/ac4a83, Crossref-verified) describes the field as digital logic wrapped around analogue cores. So the L70 Axis 1 cell must separate discrete-logic/continuous-time (digital neuromorphic) from continuous-state (memristive) designs. The reviewer's own table row ("categorically excluded from continuous temporal dynamics due to digital nature") misstates L70, which already reads "Mixed to continuous" — do not quote it. Loihi 2 specifics (Intel 4 process, 32-bit graded spikes) are unverified this session; cite the roadmap, not the reviewer's numbers.

### ✓ 2026-11-09: Three pages claim unscoped self-luminosity of conscious states ("the Map would insist — luminosity"), which Williamson's anti-luminosity argument puts under pressure at gradual boundaries
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/types-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: From `research/voids-luminosity-void-2026-10-22` (research-voids 2026-10-22 ~07:40Z). Williamson (2000, ch. 4) argues no non-trivial condition is luminous: at the edges of gradual change one can be in a state without being in a position to know it; clear cases stay known. The argument is substrate-neutral (it supports no tenet) but it constrains unscoped luminosity claims: (a) `concepts/types-of-consciousness` L67/L80 ("the Map would insist — luminosity"); (b) secondary `concepts/phenomenal-sorites-problem` L91 (68 words of headroom: length-neutral only); (c) secondary `topics/phenomenal-authority-and-first-person-evidence` L102/L140/L148 (4,519 vs topics hard 4,000, OVER: net-negative or zero-word only). Scope each claim to clear cases or flag the margin-for-error challenge in one clause; do not concede that the Map's first-person authority collapses (Merlo 2026's privileged access without luminosity is the model). GUARD: the argument concerns KNOWLEDGE of a state, not its causal role; never let it slide into epiphenomenalism. Quote Williamson only from the note's [raw]-verified strings (publisher abstracts + Srinivasan quotations); the book itself was not read. Independent of the voids-slot decision on luminosity-void (routed to the cadence/cap NEEDS-HUMAN).
